ParlaMint-PL_2018-01-09-sejm-55-1.u7	Thank you, sir. We proceed with the examination of items 1st of the agenda: Report by the Committee on Public Finance on the Government's draft budget law for 2018 (prints 1876 and 2073). I would like to ask Mr Sasin to present a report to the committee.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-01-09-sejm-55-1.u8	Thank you very much. [[Game in the O.R., bell ringing.]] Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I inform the High Chamber that on 30 September 2017 the Government of the Republic of Poland passed to the Sejm a draft budget law for 2018. The first reading of this project took place on 11 October 2017. After the Sejm has failed to accept the motion to reject the draft law at first reading, the draft law in accordance with Article. Article 106 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1306/2013 shall apply. 1 of the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm were addressed to the Committee on Public Finance and its individual parts were sent to the relevant Sejm committees to submit to the Committee on Public Finance positions containing proposals, opinions or proposals for amendments, together with justification. The draft budget law has been examined by the Committee on Public Finance in accordance with the timetable adopted by the Sejm Bureau and the result of this work is a report in print No 2073, to which 273 minority requests are attached. Before discussing the work of the Committee on Public Finances on the draft budget, the most important information on the macroeconomic assumptions adopted for the construction of the budget and the basic size of the state budget should be recalled. With regard to macroeconomic assumptions that were adopted several months ago, they can now be considered appropriate, given the recent figures of the Central Statistical Office, and the fact that the European Commission has recently increased its forecasts of growth not only in Poland but also in the European Union and in Japan. The government, when preparing the draft budget, assumed that in 2018 real GDP growth would be 3.8% , while the spring forecast of the European Commission - May 2017 - assumed GDP growth in Poland at 3.2% , whereas the macroeconomic forecasts of professional Polish forecast in June 2017 showed that GDP growth should not exceed 3.1% in 2018 up to 4.1% , so approx. 3,7% GDP according to the central forecast. The latest figures of the CSO for the three quarters of last year indicate that GDP growth may be higher than planned in 2017, i.e. higher than 3.6% in 2017. , since seasonally adjusted GDP was 4.4% in the first quarter , in the second quarter - 4,2% and in the third quarter - 5%. The macroeconomic projections of Polish professional forecasters in September 2017 indicated that GDP growth is likely to be between 3.9 and 4.2%. in 2017 and between 3,4 and 4,2% in 2018, which outlines the central scenarios for these years at 4 and 3.8% respectively. The latest autumn forecast of the European Commission also shows the following. This Decision shall enter Poland will continue to have a higher rate of growth than averages in the European Union and in the euro area, with a higher rate than in Poland being expected for only four countries of the European Union, i.e. Ireland, Malta, Slovenia and Romania. Inflation in Poland will reach 2,1% in 2018. So it's going to be lower than the Polish government started - and this assumption is 2.3%. - but at the same time higher than in the European Union, with an average of 1.7%. Inflation higher than in Poland is expected to occur only in six countries of the European Union, i.e. Estonia, Latvia, Lithuania, Hungary, Romania and the United Kingdom. Investment in the European Union will increase by 3.7% in 2018 , and in the euro area by 3.9%. In Poland this increase will be twice as high, estimated by the European Commission at almost 8%, 7.9%. Higher investment growth than in Poland is expected to have three countries of the European Union and a lower 24 countries, so Poland will be the leader when it comes to investment growth. The expected increase in investment by the Commission will be 0.3 percentage points higher than that of the Polish government, which assumed it to be 7.6%, which is associated with the acceleration of the use of EU funds from the current financial perspective 2014.2020. The faster and more efficient use of EU funds is particularly important for Poland, given the findings of the recent inspections of the Supreme Audit Chamber. The fiscal deficit of the public finance sector will amount to 1.1% in 2018 in the European Union. GDP, in euro area countries - 0.9% GDP. In Poland it is expected to reach 1.7% according to the European Commission GDP, i.e. is to be lower than the government assumed in the justification for the draft budget law for 2018. The government estimated this deficit at 2,7% GDP. In the view of the Monetary Policy Council, the deficit of the sector in 2018 may also be below the level indicated by the government. This would mean that the deficit in relation to GDP would still be lower than the limit resulting from European fiscal rules. The debt of the public finance sector will be 81% in the EU countries in 2018 GDP and 87.2% GDP in the euro area and in Poland it will be 53% GDP, so it's going to be relatively low. This means that in 2018 the sustainability of public finances will be preserved in Poland. With regard to GDP components, it can be recalled that the government assumes slightly lower export and import dynamics in 2018 than in 2017, thus maintaining a secure current account deficit of 1% GDP. At the same time, domestic demand is expected to increase by 3.9%. Returning to the assumptions adopted by the Polish government in the construction of the 2018 budget, it should be added that the labour market situation is expected to continue to improve in 2018. Average employment in the national economy is expected to be 10 457 thousand. people and the rate of unemployment recorded at the end of the period is set to fall to 6.4%. Average gross remuneration in the national economy will increase by 4.7% and will amount to PLN 4443 while average monthly pensions paid from the Social Insurance Fund will increase by 4%, i.e. to the level of PLN 2138 compared to the expected implementation of 2017 On the other hand, the average monthly agricultural pension, together with the allowances paid from KRUS, will increase to PLN 1120 and the pension - to PLN 1073. In the draft budget submitted to the Sejm for 2018, the government adopted revenue of PLN 335.7 billion, i.e. 3.5% higher compared to the expected implementation of 2017, and at the same time the forecast assumes an increase in tax revenue by 6.6%. The increase in budget revenue will mainly be influenced by the previously identified favourable macroeconomic factors and measures to rebuild the stream of tax revenue and to seal the tax system. These activities include: extending the scope of application of a single control file, i.e. better use of IT tools for detecting invoices documenting fictional activities, monitoring of road freight transport, launching the implementation of online electronic fiscal cash registers, introduction of so-called. the split payment mechanism or the introduction in CIT and PIT income tax legislation of solutions aimed at eliminating cases of application of the so-called aggressive tax optimization. State budget expenditure for 2018 has been established on the basis of the so-called. the expenditure rule contained in Article. 112aa Public Finance Act. According to it, an expenditure limit of PLN 397.2 billion was planned, i.e. an increase of PLN 12.4 billion. 3.2% in relation to the level adopted in the Budget Act in 2017. Thus, the share of expenditure in GDP will be 19.3% compared to 19.9%. in 2017. In the government bill for 2017... [[Eighteen.]] ...the funds were provided, among others.: for the financing of the scheme, the 500+ family; for the effects of reducing the retirement age to 60 for women and 65 for men; for the valorisation of pension benefits from 1 March 2018 with a rate of 102.7% ; to increase the limit on national defence expenditure, i.e. by more than PLN 3.9 billion compared to 2017; for the continuation of restructuring tasks in the hard coal mining sector; for the financing of housing tasks, including the housing package In addition, the draft law provides that the draft budget will continue to finance other tasks started in previous years, such as increasing access to pre-school education, financing regulations on free legal aid and legal education, securing funds for subsidies to agricultural fuels, and protecting vulnerable electricity consumers. In summary, it is worth noting that the 2018 draft budget will reflect the implementation of government priorities and electoral commitments. It will provide funding for the basic functions of the State while maintaining the sustainability of public finances. High Izbo! I will now go to the committee work and discuss the amendments adopted by the Committee on Public Finance. The work of the Committee on Public Finances on the draft budget took place from 25 October to 1 December 2017 in accordance with the agreed timetable. At its meetings, the Commission considered its own budgetary parts and the opinions of the other Sejm committees within the scope of the provision of the Marshal of the Sejm of 10 October 2017. During the meetings of the Committee on Public Finance, opinions were presented on the sections of the budget discussed by representatives of other committees and correspondents on behalf of the Committee on Public Finance, discussions were held, questions were asked and answers were given by government representatives and budget managers. Members also received opinions from the Sejm Analysis Office for use. During the meetings of the Committee on Public Finance, Members presented the most important information from discussions at meetings of other committees and asked a number of specific questions about the planned budgets of certain entities enjoying the high budgetary autonomy mentioned in the article. Article 139 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 2 laws on public finances, including in particular the Office of the President of the Republic of Poland, the Sejm Chancellery, the Senate Chancellery, the Ombudsman, the Supreme Chamber of Control and the Institute of National Remembrance - the Commission for the pursuit of Crime against the Polish National. Members' interest raised issues relating not only to dealing with public debt and government and local government debt in the future, but also to issues relating to the level of planned lower contributions to the State budget from State Treasury dividends. The answers received showed, among other things, that the Treasury does not expect dividends to be paid, but rather that the realised profits should remain in the Treasury companies and be invested by them. Lower dividends are also the result of lower profits of companies as a result of international competition, which is caused inter alia. import of artificial fertilisers from Russia. It is worth noting, however, that many of the issues raised in the discussions in relation to the policies of the State Treasury companies should have been discussed in the Commission for Energy and the State Treasury. Some Members were concerned about the level of remuneration in some individuals, including the fact that they were concerned. in the Agency for Restructuring and Modernization of Agriculture, or the existence of wage disparities, e.g. in the control bodies between the Supreme Audit Chamber and the RIO and the State Labour Inspection, or any other type of disparities, e.g. between courts and prosecutors, whether in terms of the proportion of financing expenditure from national and EU sources in agriculture or the financing of sport for children and young people through State special-purpose funds. This discussion also asked for a reduction in funds for the State Plant Protection and Seed Protection Inspection or insufficient expenditure of the State Fund for the Rehabilitation of Persons with Disabilities, according to the debaters. These issues have been clarified in detail and it can in principle be pointed out that some of the problems have been resolved by amending the 2017 budget and the amendments tabled to the 2018 draft budget, as well as by amending the around budget laws. This included, among others, the following: increase the expenditure of the State Plant and Seed Protection Inspections or expenditure on the prosecutor's office, as well as expenditure on culture and the protection of monuments. Some of the concerns of Members could be considered unfounded in cases where expenditure planned by the government in 2018 for certain areas, e.g. for agriculture, physical culture, science as well as PFRON are higher than in 2017. Members were also interested in infrastructure expenditure, especially road expenditure. The heated discussion in this area highlighted the need for better preparation of detailed information by the Ministry of Infrastructure and Construction. The information provided by the Ministry's representative indicated that the total expenditure planned for 2018 amounts to PLN 37.3 billion at PLN 27 billion in 2017. This, of course, concerns the infrastructure and construction department, as I was just talking about. The allocation for national roads in the current financial perspective is at the same level as in the previous perspective, i.e. PLN 40 billion, although in the current financial perspective the co-financing calculation system was changed from so-called. individual gaps on the so-called a flat-rate gap, i.e. the departure from funding converted by a complex algorithm for each single project to a solution in which for each project EU funding amounts to around 50%. The expenditure for the entire national road construction programme increased from PLN 107 billion in 2017 to PLN 135 billion, and the distribution of this expenditure over the years results from suggestions from the road industry. In addition, with regard to the issue of the financing of district roads or subsidies or the co-financing of local roads, the Ministry indicated that for the year 2018 the amount will be maintained as in 2017, but that the Ministry is working on a new source of financing for these roads, it wants the Fund for Local Roads to operate in this area. The discussion also concerned the housing programme. Here this discussion was also of a historical nature, referring to those programmes that had been in operation in previous years. There have also been discussions about the National Water Farm - Polish Waters - it is already within the scope of the other resort - and the financial possibilities of this new entity, which will manage Polish waters. There were many topics in the discussion and, of course, it is impossible to mention all those that have emerged in this debate on the budget. It should only be said that part of the answer was also given to Members by written means. With regard to the work of the committees to which the draft budget law was intended to be examined in the area of their competence, it is worth recalling that the Sejm committees have submitted a total of 26 opinions to the Committee on Public Finance, with a large majority of the committees not raising any objections to the draft budget law, accepting the Government's submission. Only some committees have put forward proposals for amendments, which in total were not much, which they were about to discuss. A total of 409 amendments were tabled to the draft budget law for 2018, including two amendments concerning the textual part. Among the amendments relating to the annexes to the budget: 143 amendments concerned the revenue of the State budget included in Annex 1 and indicated the need to raise tax revenue, mainly from the tax on goods and services, in order to finance the specific expenditure indicated in those amendments; 259 amendments concerned the expenditure of the State budget included in Annex 2 and these were mainly amendments reducing one expenditure in order to ensure that the other expenditure indicated in those amendments could be financed; six amendments concerned amendments to the other annexes. The committees have tabled 19 amendments and have attached five separate sentences to their opinions. Members tabled 385 amendments, most of which were tabled by Members of the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club. On the day of the votes, one amendment concerning the text was withdrawn and only 67 amendments, including one to the text, were accepted by the committee. The Commission has not accepted 341 amendments, the vast majority of which have been tabled again as minority proposals. The Commission did not accept any of the separate sentences or amendments on which the representative of the government, Minister Teresa Czerwińska, expressed a negative opinion. The Minister's reservations raised in particular those amendments where an increase in the revenue of the State budget from VAT under part 77 was indicated as a source of financing for additional expenditure, a reduction in expenditure on the handling of the Treasury debt under part 79 and a reduction in the appropriations under the special-purpose reserve in item 79. 8 is the reserve for co-financing projects involving European funds. Thus, the proposed amendments provided for an increase in budget revenue of almost PLN 10 billion, including VAT of more than PLN 9 billion and thus significantly interfered with the revenue of the budget, without proposing changes in the tax system resulting in an effective increase in revenue. In this context, it was also recalled that the budget was only based on discretionary actions, a sum of PLN 10.7 billion was planned. Another group of amendments indicated as a source of funding - for a total amount of more than PLN 3.3 billion - appropriations from Part 79, which provides for expenditure on the handling of the Treasury debt. Debt management expenditure shall comply with the provisions of the Public Finance Act and shall be priority expenditure. They are already largely determined. They are of such a nature in the budget law and therefore cannot be reduced. It is also worth recalling that in relation to GDP, the cost of handling debt will decrease to 1.49% in 2018 GDP. Amendments indicating as a source of funding the special-purpose reserve in item. 8 would significantly reduce this reserve - for a total amount of PLN 4.3 billion, i.e. they would absorb about 80% expenditure included in that reserve. This reserve is intended for national co-financing in the current financial perspective. Experience with the implementation of the previous financial perspective 2007-2013 shows that 2018 should be a groundbreaking year in terms of expenditure on European funds. By comparing the timetable or structure of expenditure over time, in the same year, i.e. in 2011, almost PLN 43 billion was spent under the Structural Funds, which constituted 15% the total allocation of funds allocated to Poland. If this amount relates to the current financial perspective, the expenditure should amount to about PLN 50 billion for the Structural Funds themselves. Therefore, given some delays in the implementation of the programmes in the current perspective, this amount should be even higher and the resources in the reserve are planned to amount to PLN 4.95 billion. In addition, Minister Teresa Czerwińska, a representative of the government, pointed out that some of the amendments were not legal. For example, the amendments concerning the reduction of expenditure in Part 29: National defence, i.e. the reduction of expenditure, which would de facto result in a reduction in the appropriations spent on defence in 2018, which would violate the provision of the Article. 7 Act of 25 May 2001 on technical reconstruction and modernisation and financing of the Armed Forces of the Republic of Poland. According to this provision, expenditure from the State budget of not less than 2% shall be allocated annually for the financing of defence needs. GDP. As far as the other key sources of funding are concerned, the target reserve was indicated in item 1. 83: Funding for organizational tasks in Poland Exhibitions EXPO 2022 in Łódź. In this item, the total amount proposed in the amendments to the reduction of this reserve was PLN 276.1 million. However, with regard to this reserve, it should be noted that a vote took place at the seat of the Organisation for Economic Cooperation and Development in Paris at the 162nd session of the International Bureau of Exhibition on 15 November 2017, which decided to transfer the right to the Expo organisation in 2022 to Argentina. As the government, on the initiative of the Minister of Development and Finance, secured the necessary financial resources related to Poland's application for the international Expo 2022 exhibition in Łódź, there was an opportunity to release or develop funds of this reserve in the total amount of PLN 360.3 million for additional tasks, including tasks financed by individual voivodships. Many of the amendments were just about transferring funds to the voivodships for the implementation of the tasks set out in the amendments. However, the Minister pointed out that in view of the lack of possibility to allocate the appropriations for the budgetary classification tasks in this vast number of amendments, it was possible to include them in the reserve envisaged under Article. The Commission shall adopt implementing acts laying down detailed rules for the implementation of this Regulation in accordance with Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council laying down detailed rules for the application of this Regulation. 4 Public Finance Act in parts of the state budget, which is governed by individual voivodships. As the regulation of this article resulted in a reduction in the level, the planned expenditure under the reserve to 1% expenditure excluding grants to local government units, it has become necessary to amend the budget law for 2018, i.e. exemption from the application of Article. The Commission shall adopt implementing acts laying down detailed rules for the implementation of this Regulation in accordance with Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council laying down detailed rules for the application of this Regulation. The 4th Public Finance Act. This solution allows for the financing of tasks in a flexible manner in different areas of public activity, of course under and after the applicable procedures and regulations. High Izbo! As far as 500+ is concerned, the drop in the number of families receiving social assistance was around 12%. So we see it clearly, as I say. In 2018, these numbers will be even clearer, but we still need to show these. Mrs Zofia Czernow asked about the decrease in social rehabilitation expenditure in PFRON. There is no decrease in expenditure in PFRON, there is an increase in expenditure in PFRON. The draft law for 2018 provides for expenditure of 5 021 248 thousand, i.e. 140 million more than in 2017. We have more expenses in all our positions. As regards the increase in funding for salaries, there is an increase of 150 million, i.e. up to PLN 3150 million. In terms of socio-occupational rehabilitation, local governments also receive more resources. The budget plan allocates 17 million more to the counties, i.e. up to 780 million this year. This is also an increase. The amount for tasks commissioned for NGOs increased from PLN 190 million in 2017 to PLN 247 million in 2018. As regards the specific figures, in 2015 local governments received funds from PFRON amounting to 914 million, in 2016-920, in 2017-930, in 2018-947 million. Mr Szczerba asked for the funds to be secured for the implementation of the Constitutional Tribunal judgment on the financing of care services, depending on the degree of disability, to compensate for these benefits. This judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal, of course, is being examined. There are two teams working right now. One team is a diagnosis team for the situation of people with disabilities. The result of this team's work is to recommend to the Prime Minister how to improve the situation of carers of people with disabilities. There is also an inter-ministerial review and drafting team on case law. There, too, work is to be completed in March and recommendations to the government will be prepared. This trial team is very important because we are not able to determine how many disabled or independent people need the care of other people, carers. As former chair of the Senior Committee, Mr Szczerba knows best that the number of older people, over 100 years of age, over 90 years of age, over 80 years of age, who require help and are independent, is growing, so in fact the caselaw here is crucial to actually refer to disability as well as independence, because we have a lot of older people who also need care. I'm sure the subject isn't on the shelf, we're working on it, we're analyzing it. We also have the first fittings for legal solutions, but here, as I say, it is crucial that we can determine which persons need carers, how many carers they need, and how many carers should receive a benefit that would give them support when they give up their work and take care of the person in need of support. Just a little more patience. I hope that in March we will be able to show the results of the teams that deal with case law. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

We proceed with the examination of item 2 of the agenda: First reading of the government draft law on electromobility and alternative fuels (print No. 2147). ParlaMint-PL_2018-01-09-sejm-55-1.u132	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, I have the honour to present a draft law on electromobility and alternative fuels, 2147 Seym printing. The aim of the project is to stimulate and support the development of electromobility and the use of other alternative fuels in the transport sector in Poland. Electricity-based transport is a new, fast-growing trend, which includes more and more countries, and the electromobility market alone allows to create completely new solutions and new business models. The integration of Poland into changes taking place at global level will allow optimal use of them for both the economic development of the country and for improving the quality of the environment in Poland, in particular in urban agglomerations and densely populated areas, where the negative impact of transport on air quality is most felt. We could have done that, for example. today feel in a city like Warsaw. Polish agglomerations are too often mentioned as negative examples, and the electromobility Act is one of the instruments of government intervention in this area. I hope that the bill will be an incentive to build charging infrastructure for electric vehicles and, consequently, to change the structure of fuels used in transport towards alternative fuels with significantly lower emissions. If you would allow me to develop the above, I would like to highlight three significant advantages of the development of electromobility and alternative fuels. Firstly, it is a significant civilization and technological revolution. Business and technological solutions related to electromobility will benefit both the Polish energy sector, the development of the transport sector as well as the industrial sector, in particular the automotive sector, telecommunications and ICT companies. I think it is not necessary to repeat that Poles have very large achievements in these areas, and we want, by becoming part of the most innovative trend of technological change at this moment in the telecommunications sector, to take advantage of this potential and to give the most entrepreneurial and creative Poles a chance. Well, so that they can exist in this trend. We believe that as a result of this law, there will be an increase in enterprise innovation and the development of research into new technologies. Already today in the field of electromobility we can boast of many Polish brands known and valued in Europe and the world. Secondly, it should be borne in mind that Poland has small oil reserves and is therefore dependent on its imports. The choice of electricity as fuel in transport is a strategic choice that improves our energy security. If today Poland imports around 97% oil, that means that annually it is transferred from Poland abroad between 0.2 and 0.4% Polish GDP. As far as import volumes are concerned, between 3, 4, 5 and even 9% Polish imports were just purchases of oil. These are not only significant values in absolute terms, but also their variability is important, in this sense the entry into electromobility allows us to significantly reduce this volatility. If we are talking about absolute values, these are expenditures of USD 10 and 20 billion per year, and in terms of volatility, this is a risk associated with changes in energy and oil prices, which Poland has no impact on. By developing the electromobility market, we will reduce our dependence on oil imports and strengthen energy security. Thirdly, finally, and this is something that I have already mentioned before, the bill could significantly improve the air quality in cities. Transport is a significant source of nitrogen oxides, PM10 dust and PM2.5 dust and benzopyrene emissions. It also contributes to CO2 emissions. In Poland, the situation is particularly critical - in terms of PM10, PM2.5 and benzopyrene pollution. In large agglomerations there is also a problem of air pollution with ozone and nitrogen oxides. The development of electric transport is one of the many activities undertaken in this area. It is precisely because we can take such actions, which result from the Act on Electromobility, the prospect of improving air quality in such a city, e.g. like Warsaw, where 63% of pollution comes from transport, will become - I believe - a real perspective. It should also be added that the proposed law transposes the Directive of the European Parliament and of the Council 2014/94 of 22 October 2014 on the development of alternative fuel infrastructure into the Polish legal order. The project has been the subject of a very intensive consultation, intensive dialogue, both with business and local partners, as a result of which over 100 institutions have been directly involved in the dialogue and consultation of this law over the last year. We received about a thousand pages of comments. It was a really intense consultation process, which included not only the exchange of correspondence, but also dozens of workshops, seminars and meetings. As a result of this process - I can say - the ambition of electromobility becomes the ambition that unites us all, because the challenges we face are the same. High Izbo! The proposed law is based on two main assumptions: firstly, the use, as far as possible, of market mechanisms in the infrastructure segment, secondly, a comprehensive approach to the development of the alternative fuel segment by building incentives for the exchange of low- and zero-carbon car fleets. In particular, the draft law lays down the principles for the construction of a core infrastructure network for alternative fuels to be established in an initial phase in urban agglomerations, in densely populated areas and along the roads belonging to the trans-European transport corridors. The bill in this context focuses on electric vehicle charging stations and natural gas stations. In our opinion, these types of alternative fuels require regulation at the level of the law in the current technological development, but at the same time such an approach allows optimal use of the conditions of the Polish market and energy policy. The expansion of the core network will allow the free movement of alternative fuels-based vehicles. The core network will create 6400 charging points for electricity and 70 CNG natural gas stations. The proposed law introduces into the Polish legal order the basic concepts necessary for the development of the market and infrastructure of alternative fuels, because one of the barriers that were identified during the preliminary work on the project was the lack of normative definitions. This legal gap has often caused problems in obtaining appropriate approvals and permits. The introduction of an appropriate conceptual spectrum and its sequencing facilitates many investments and activities in alternative fuels. A key element of this part of the project is the definition of the path of development and construction of public charging stations for electric vehicles and filling points for natural gas. As a result of our project, the infrastructure for charging electric vehicles in the first period, from 2018 to 2020, is to be developed on the basis of market principles. However, it should be stressed that stakeholders will be able to apply for public funding. Moreover, the draft law proposes significant facilitations for entities investing in infrastructure development, among others. no building permit, free parking at charging stations by the municipality. Municipalities, which are large urban agglomerations, will be obliged to monitor the development of infrastructure and actively engage in infrastructure development planning. Where the results of the monitoring indicate that the development of infrastructure is not sufficiently dynamic, i.e. the number of public charging points in a given municipality will not correspond to the minimum number laid down in the proposed law, the authorities of such municipality will be required to prepare an infrastructure development plan. Then the operator of the electricity distribution network will be responsible for the construction of charging points. The arrangement of the development process of these public charging stations in the proposed way will allow private or public entities to deploy charging stations in locations suitable for their investment plans without an administrative order. On the other hand, with regard to the development of natural gas infrastructure, a network of core natural gas charging station infrastructure is planned. This network will be limited to municipalities fulfilling the specific conditions set out in the Act. However, the designer's approach is prescriptive and administrative in nature because of the nature of the natural gas market used in transport. Even a small number of vehicles powered by this fuel cause less interest of investors in building adequate infrastructure. On the other hand, the provisions of Directive 2014/94/EU have made it clear that for the development of the alternative fuel market it is necessary to deploy infrastructure, including, in the case of Poland, infrastructure for natural gas. The project also lays down the rules for the operation of the above mentioned. the infrastructure and indicate the bodies responsible for the construction and management of charging stations and natural gas stations. This should enable the establishment of a core infrastructure network for these fuels and thus contribute to the achievement of the objectives set by the designer. The project defines rules on how to inform consumers about alternative fuels, how to label distributors and vehicles and how to create and operate Alternative Fuel Infrastructure Records. The draft law will also allow municipalities to introduce clean transport zones. However, it should be noted that the decision to introduce zones and their functioning will remain the responsibility of local authorities. This will allow zones to be adapted to local circumstances, while at the same time respecting the autonomy of municipalities and local communities. The bill provides for a number of benefits for drivers of electric vehicles, among others. exemption from excise duty on the purchase of electric vehicles and hydrogen-powered vehicles, a more favourable depreciation rate for electric vehicles, the possibility of moving electric vehicles on road lanes for buses. These incentives will support the purchase and use of electric vehicles. Finally, it is worth noting that this project is an invitation for entrepreneurs and innovative ones. We have included a number of very interesting solutions concerning, among others. the ability to experiment when it comes to monorail, magnetic transport. We have also included solutions for testing autonomous vehicles. For the first time in Polish legislation there will be provisions for testing autonomous vehicles. We all know that if we think about what the city will look like in 10, 20, 30 years from now, we will have to consider the existence of innovative transport solutions that technology already makes possible today, but the regulation does not keep up with it yet. If Poland does not implement such regulations, which will entail entrepreneurship, creativity, innovation, we will not be able to stand in the way of the best in the world, we will not be able to co-form civilization trends, and we will only be the recipient of these trends. The proposals contained in this Act are intended to allow the development of the alternative fuels market, in particular those specified in the title of that Act on electromobility. Participation in this technological revolution related to electromobility, with the development of electric vehicles, will allow Poland to create new industries, technologically advanced jobs, to achieve economic growth. At the same time, we will ensure energy security and clean air in cities. The whole world is already moving towards electromobility and I am convinced that Poland must also choose this direction. The project presented is an important, fundamental step in this direction. From the perspective of the Minister of Energy, I would like to add that this is the third constitutional act creating a new market. After the Energy Law was introduced in 1997 and the renewable energy law defined a new market, now the Act on Electromobility and Alternative Fuels creates such a new economic space for development. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! In passing on the project to be considered, I would like to assure you that the Government Party is ready to participate in the parliamentary work on it. I believe that this regulation will allow Poland to participate in the technological revolution on a non-recipient basis, but co-create global trends. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

Such a stage in the development of this market makes there is no adequate infrastructure for fuelling, loading vehicles using these fuels. The provisions of the proposed law cover mainly the two types of alternative fuels - electricity and natural gas. These fuels now have the greatest potential for development. It should also be pointed out that the draft law transposes the Directive of the European Parliament and of the Council of 22 October 2014 on the development of alternative fuels infrastructure into the Polish legal order. This Directive requires Member States to develop alternative fuel infrastructure. States should ensure the development of charging points for electric vehicles, CNG or LNG filling points and LNG bunkering points for vessels. The Directive also requires Member States to introduce technical specifications, harmonised rules for charging electric vehicles and consumer information. The proposed law aims to define the principles for the development and functioning of infrastructure for the use of key alternative fuels in transport, including infrastructure used in public collective transport, public entities' obligations relating to the development of the electromobility market, information obligations on alternative fuels, conditions for the operation of low-carbon transport zones, and principles for the creation and content of a national policy framework for the development of alternative fuels infrastructure. One of the most important parts of the proposed changes is the definition of the path of development and construction of public charging stations for electric vehicles and natural gas filling points. The project also lays down the rules for the operation of the above mentioned. the infrastructure and indicate the bodies responsible for the construction and management of charging stations and natural gas stations. This should enable the establishment of a core network of these fuels infrastructure and thus contribute to the achievement of the objectives of the law and the objectives of the national framework set by the designer. The project submitted envisages separate paths for building infrastructure for electricity and natural gas. As the electromobility market is a new market, the rules for its functioning need to be regulated. Therefore, rules and arrangements have been identified to define the role, privileges and responsibilities of individual operators in this market. The proposed law also provides for the possibility for municipalities to introduce clean transport zones on which only alternative fuel vehicles could move. The introduction of zones will not be mandatory, but may facilitate the fight against air pollution in cities. The project also sets out rules on how to inform consumers about alternative fuels, how to label distributors and vehicles and how to establish and operate the records of alternative fuels infrastructure. This is a requirement of the directive I mentioned earlier. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Parliamentary Club Law and Justice requests that this project be directed to further work. Thank you very much. ParlaMint-PL_2018-01-09-sejm-55-1.u155	Thank you very much. Please, Mr Ireneusz Gains, near Free and Solidarnia.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-01-09-sejm-55-1.u156	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! On behalf of the Freedom and Solidarnia Circle, I present my position on the government draft law on electromobility and alternative fuels. The bill was long awaited because of the need to include national climate policy in the framework of European Union directives and the global energy transition. The bill was subject to special arrangements and public consultation. As a result of these actions, a legislative act has been created, which is a compromise between the administrative burden for citizens and the necessary regulations creating a space for building a new clean environment that is free from toxic chemicals and dust in the air around us. One of the means to increase the climate neutrality of transport and improve the state of the economy in terms of applying environmental solutions is the development of the market for alternative fuels, which is in the initial stage of development in Poland. It is therefore necessary to establish a legal framework in line with the requirements of Directive 2014/94/EU for the development of alternative fuels infrastructure, for the filling of natural gas vehicles, for the identification of information obligations on alternative fuels and for the establishment of rules for the operation of clean transport zones. The second pillar of the implementation of the aforementioned. The European Union directive is to create conditions for the development of electromobility, including the definition of rules for the provision of electric vehicle charging services. This draft law sets out separate paths for the creation of infrastructure for electricity and natural gas. This approach is due to the differences between these two types of fuels. The aim of the proposed act is also to develop public charging stations for electric vehicles and filling stations for natural gas cars throughout Poland. Although the contested law imposes new obligations on gas and electricity distribution system operators and other operators in these markets, their entry into force is however spread over time and will serve the good of the whole society. Some controversy in public opinion raises the possibility for municipalities to introduce clean transport zones, where only alternative fuel-powered vehicles can move. However, it should be noted that the introduction of these zones will not be mandatory and will certainly facilitate the fight against air pollution in cities. The whole conceptual apparatus and a coherent catalogue of definitions concerning natural gas and electromobility infrastructure should be welcomed in the draft law. The introduction of statutory technical requirements for charging points will allow the proper implementation of the provisions of Directive 2014/94/EU and ensure the safety of electric vehicle users. A positive solution is to exclude the economic activity of offering a vehicle charging service from the obligation to have a concession for the sale of electricity, which should interest small operators in this new activity. As part of the drafting of the bill, around 1200 comments were submitted by almost 100 different entities representing often different positions. A large group of entrepreneurs, electricity distribution system operators as well as local government units and electric vehicle users themselves were among those who submitted comments. The results of the public consultation, together with their analysis, have been presented, inter alia. at the meeting of the Parliamentary Mining and Energy Team on 26 October 2017, a parliamentary team which I have the honour to be president. It is also worth noting that one of the important elements supporting and complementary the development of the electromobility system will now be the final phase of the agreement of the Minister of Energy on detailed rules for the shaping and calculation of tariffs and electricity billing, called the Anti-Smog Tariff Regulation, which will encourage not only the use of electric heating in households but also the charging of electric vehicles at night, i.e. from 3 p.m. 22 to 10 p.m. 6. According to the logic of the total opposition, it is best not to do anything, [[♪ The bell ♪]] Therefore, today's criticism expressed by Members of the Civic Platform or the Polish People's Party. That is why I would like to stress with all my power here that this is a very good bill. [[I think you were asleep.]] One reservation, Minister. I refer to the statements made by my previous speakers, especially Mr Sitarski from Kukiz.15. Please draw your attention and enter a record in the Act on the use of hydrogen as fuel. I think this supplement is necessary.

From my perspective, I can assure you that the Ministry of Energy has had a very intense dialogue with other resorts, including in particular the finance department, and the work effect we have is a balanced compromise between where we are today in terms of budget, and where technological development is today in terms of electromobility. I would like to refer here to the greatest ally of this project. This is technological progress. This is the factor that makes electric vehicles cheaper and cheaper every year. And if we now rely on research or analysis concerning not only Poland... In her last speech, she asked the question about whether we were based on some kind of study. Well, there are a lot of them, there are more and more of them, and they generally indicate that there will be a structural change around 2020, 2022, in this period of time, in terms of the cost structure of the use of the vehicle based on the combustion engine versus the electric vehicle. This is why technological progress is the biggest ally of this project. In our thinking about electromobility, we have been constantly looking for ways that will enable this technological progress to stimulate or build conditions in which it could best affect the structure of costs. And if you could ask me, I would ask that this set of support instruments, which was included in the Act on Electromobility, be treated not selectively, speaking of excise duties only or only one or another element, but comprehensively. Because from my perspective this law is a kind of incentive, it is a kind of toolkit for entrepreneurial and innovative people who will be able to build innovative business models from various components of this law. For example, we can think of carsharing operators here. We did special workshops with them in Gdynia, at the turn of the year... in recent weeks, on how we can take into account the challenges of carsharing in electromobility, because carsharing is a way to reduce the number of vehicles in the city. At the same time, we wondered how to make these vehicles greener and more electric. And what they paid attention to, e.g. Carsharing operators, the fact that the key barriers for them today as regards the use of vehicles in cities is, for example, non-accessibility of parking spaces. Hence, we give local governments the opportunity to decide where parking spaces for electric vehicles will be dedicated. If they are additionally equipped with charging infrastructure, it is all the better, but you can also imagine only dedicated places for electric vehicles, plus free parking in city centers, if all of these seats are occupied, the possibility of using lanes reserved for buses. This is why fiscal incentives must be imposed, so it is about excise duty exemption or depreciation. The depreciation copy, which is proposed in this Act, is very important because it changes the amount of the write-off from 20 to 30 thousand. Euros. In other words, the trader will be able to include in the cost of using such a vehicle additionally 10,000. Euros. And in this context, with regard to this set of tools or a set of instruments that are mentioned in the Act, I would ask whether I would encourage in this discussion we to treat it comprehensively and to treat it as a sort of set of instruments that will then be creatively worked out by entrepreneurs. And in this context, I would like to say that the situation is not static, that the electromobility market poses completely new development challenges for us. It's not like we can go to some country and see how they do it, and obviously better or worse to copy only what's there. Many countries today are experimenting with different types of models in terms of electromobility as regards autonomous transport. And we too will have to face the fact that such legislation should be periodically reviewed, should be periodically assessed as to whether all the innovative business models that form in economic space are able to absorb regulatoryly. So that as far as this bill is concerned, its simplicity is like simplicity, I would say, a constitution, because it is giving simple, predictable, clear rules as to how this market should be constructed. It clearly gives priority to market action in terms of electromobility, because we expect business initiatives here. However, we are certainly also aware that during this first period we will need additional support. It was here - I will now try to address the questions that have been asked me - on the part of Mr Arndt, the question concerning the amount of 60 million from the state budget. This is the amount of direct payments, while the total cost of this law consists of a lot of elements, including the depreciation write-downs mentioned by me. Or an exemption from excise duty. And it all consists of an amount that exceeds PLN 18 billion during the duration of this law. On the other hand, these expenditures from the State budget, in respect of the years 2018, 2019, are already included in the Budget Act or relate to 2019. I would also like to add that by learning this electromobility or creating public dynamics, public policy around electromobility, we have just started with public transport. And in this context, at the end of last year, from the point of view of the government, there were two very important events: on the one hand, on 28 December there was the adoption of this draft law, which we have the opportunity to discuss today, but on the other hand, 29 December there was the signing of an agreement between the National Fund for Environmental Protection and Water Management and the National Centre for Research and Development on the financing of a public transport emission-free programme, which mobilises public funds of over PLN 2 billion for the mobilisation of this public electric transport. This is an unprecedented powerful programme, and it seems to me that it is certainly the largest in Europe, the question whether, at this moment, if left aside China, not one of the largest in the world, which on this scale puts on the development of electromobility, and on this scale says that as a country we are ready, where this market is most mature, put sufficient resources on the line to make this revolution our industry benefit from, our economy benefit, our technical thought, and in the perspective of being able to top this glass ceiling of the medium income trap. As regards issues related to the project - Mrs Kołacz-Leszczyńska's question - whether it is limited to the main roads and motorways, I would say that it is not. This project is absolutely not limited to the main roads and motorways, but provides that in 32 major agglomerations in Poland, as well as along European routes, transport corridors should be sufficient infrastructure, which is defined by us in the documents that were adopted at the beginning of 2017, in March 2017. This is a document that talks about the development of alternative fuel infrastructure, where we have found that the minimum we need to talk about is 6400 charging points, which will arise in these major agglomerations and on the main roads, but in no way limits anything, but on the contrary. As far as the Polish automotive sector is concerned, because here a question was asked about Polish electric cars, there is undoubtedly Polish potential of the automotive industry, i.e. over 1000 companies are very dynamically developing, with huge innovation potential. There is a dialogue between the Ministry of Energy and these actors, so I can say that our government dynamics regarding regulation, as well as the promotion of electromobility, are not unrelated to their business plans. These companies are aware that in order to benefit from this revolution, they must take action today to adapt their offer and be competitive for those who will want to install electric vehicles in the future. Now it is important to be aware that the automotive industry is, generally speaking, a global industry, so it is becoming increasingly difficult to distinguish between the place where the vehicle is mounted and the network of subsuppliers. As Poland, we are also part of such sub-supply networks and what we can talk about today is the aim of ensuring that the elements of this manufacturing chain of electric vehicles with the highest added value are located in Poland. From this perspective, the emphasis on electromobility, the promotion of electromobility really does not remain without echo from the perspective of the business plans of these entities. But then, when it comes to whether it is possible to say that the part is constructed here and another - elsewhere, I think it is a less critical issue. We have had a lot of comments on the hydrogen issue - Mr Sitarski, Mr Papke - for which I thank you. Indeed, hydrogen technologies certainly have a future, which is also reflected in this law. We defined a hydrogen vehicle, we considered that hydrogen vehicles should also benefit from a number of these benefits, which were defined in the Act. However, we must also be aware that the development of these markets, in terms of electromobility versus hydrogen, is taking place at different paces and that we must once again pragmatically direct our attention to those areas that are developing as soon as possible, which, as I said, does not mean that the hydrogen issue should not be addressed in any way. We're moving her in this bill. Moreover, within the framework of a fund relating to low-carbon transport - this is a law which accompanies the electromobility Act, which is a source of funding for many initiatives that have been defined in this law - will also be an opportunity to finance hydrogen-related activities. As regards issues relating to Mr Cieślinski's questions on whether the project contains sufficient relief for citizens, I would say that, as I mentioned, this project is a balanced compromise reached within the framework of the government and lists a number of benefits for citizens, including. excise duty, the ability to move around the bus lanes, free parking. However, we should also consider the relationship between the cost of buying such a vehicle and the cost of operating it. It turns out that the electric vehicle is much cheaper in operation, especially now that, as the Ministry of Energy, we are taking initiatives to reduce the cost of charging the electric vehicle, in general the cost of electricity at night. This cost will be within a few zlotys for driving 100 km. In other words, the electric vehicle will be much cheaper to use for an individual user, who - I am convinced - will also see the advantages of this vehicle over time. More and more of our citizens will be convinced of electric vehicles already at the beginning, as the cost of buying them decreases, but above all to a significant extent afterwards, looking at how low the cost of using them will be compared to the cost of using combustion vehicles. Referring to Mr Arkadiusz Marchewka's question, I will say that 51 cities have already signed letters of intent in terms of cooperation between the government and local governments on the development of electromobility. In 2017 there were 79 electric buses in Poland. We estimate that in 2019 there will be 430 of them, which is a huge increase. As for the 100 electric buses that we produced in 2017, in 2018 - as all the data that we collect from manufacturers at the moment - the increase will be three times, we will produce 300% more of these vehicles, there will be 300 in 2018. Ideally we shot this offer into the needs of not only the Polish but also the European self-government market. Thank you very much for reminding Mr Kasprzak of the monorail issue. This is actually a topic that specifically concerns a city such as the Reichs. I hope that the bill, which we are now adopting, will at least open the possibility to experiment, test such solutions, because testing such solutions will be necessary to develop implementing rules for this law. One thing is to create at the level of the law the possibility of using monorails, and the other is the implementing rules, which, as I say, as in many cases of technological development, we will have to learn a little bit more, so here is a kind of synergy to predict when it comes to R &amp; D expenditure. With regard to Mr Meysztowicz's question about CO2 produced by electric vehicles, as well as throughout the entire production chain of electric vehicles, this is undoubtedly an area for analysis, for discussion. I think there will certainly be a lot going on here as regards what is the overall balance sheet as regards the various types of component production. However, what we can say today and there are research results of European institutions, but also European think tanks, what is very important, is that in the case of the Polish power mix, it is still largely a mix, which also contains a lot of components, but above all it is a mix based on traditional fossil fuels, regardless of which CO2 emissions from such a vehicle are already significantly lower than emissions from a direct combustion vehicle. We therefore benefit not only from what I think is crucial from the perspective of Polish cities, as regards nitrogen oxides, as regards all types of dust, PM10, PM2,5, because this is what is bothering us today in the centers of cities, but also from the point of view of what is inventory in European climate policy, namely CO2 emissions. I think we had some more questions... [[Mr Agnieszka Kołacz-Leszczyńska: About the support fund...]] ...on the part of Mr Gadowski, Mr Kobyliński, on the replacement of import cars, on the time to change the fleet in local governments and administrations. With regard to Mr Owl's question about how networks are being developed, how infrastructure is developed by fuel companies, indeed all fuel companies are already taking up the challenge of electromobility, both Orlen and Lotos. The cost of storing documentation in the year was estimated at PLN 130 million. This change is expected by society and I think it will also reduce the grey area in terms of labour salaries. They aim to eliminate employees' records that operate without obtaining an entry in the register of holders of personal and payroll files kept by the Marshal of the voivodship. Documents archived by unauthorised keepers shall not constitute evidence in the pension or pension proceedings which may result in a reduction in the size of the benefit. ParlaMint-PL_2018-01-09-sejm-55-1.u202	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! On behalf of the Poselski Club I would like to present a statement on the report of the committee on the government draft law amending certain laws in connection with the shortening of the period of storage of employee files and their electronicisation, in print Nos 1995 and 2119. The project concerns the reduction from 50 to 10 years of retention of employees' records and payment records by transferring to the Social Security Office the data necessary to grant and determine the amount of pensions and to enable the employer to keep and maintain employee records in electronic form, optionally, which will simplify the handling of personal files. On 13 December 2017, the Extraordinary Commission for the deregulation has been working on this project and has not made any amendments to it. As the drafters point out in the explanatory memorandum, these provisions will speed up the process of drafting documents, shorten the search time of the files and, as a result, improve the quality of the documentation and the cost of storage will be reduced. The proposed solutions will have a positive impact on the safety of the employee. Among other things, he will not have to prove the correctness of the data recorded in his account before the ZUS. This will eliminate in the future the need to obtain from the former employer e.g. employment or salary certificates. In addition, the solutions so far are financial to employers. The long-term storage of employee records generates costs of about PLN 130 million per year for employers. However, the bill raises doubts in several points. Firstly, as noted by the National Trade Unions Agreement, the draft law does not indicate the obligation to apply safe hardware storage conditions. The OPZZ considers that it is appropriate to leave the existing form of information storage, as despite technological progress it is the most convenient and transparent not only for the parties to the employment relationship, but also for the authorities controlling the application and compliance of the law at the workplace. It is also indicated that storage of employees' documents in electronic form may be exposed to a number of negative factors. It belongs to them, among others. loss of data in case of breakdown of information systems or network hacking. Concerns also arise due to the inability to use equipment and programs, which can lead to negative effects for the employee, of course, but also for the employer. Despite these doubts, which we maintain all the time, because it is worth listening to the social side, trade unions, people who work every day in businesses, employers, despite those reservations which I hope will, in the course of the work still in Parliament, not only in the Seym, but also in the Senate, be disbanded, clarified, Modern is in favour of further work on this project, in favour of adopting this project, facilitating the lives of entrepreneurs, employers, but also employees, employees. We believe that the improvement of deregulation will benefit both sides of the employment relationship. We would like to draw attention to the fact that the doubts raised in the course of the public consultation should be resolved in the course of the parliamentary work or possibly in the Senate. So, at the very end, I'm going to repeat: we are in favour of this bill. I hope that the law will come into force quickly and will make life easier for entrepreneurs, employers, but also for employees in Poland. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

Just before 1999, the employer provided only aggregated information on the amount of contributions for all employees, without being able to assign them to individual insured persons. Therefore, this proposal can certainly not include this dossier because it is not possible to verify this. On the other hand, when it comes to the task of trying to clean up these files, which, I say, are lost or stored by the insecure, I will say euphemistically, the keepers, this is the problem, by answering the questions of Mr Wilczyński and Mrs Hrynkiewicz, we want to deal with the president of the Social Security Office with you. Mr Meysztowicz asked about the situation of closure of the company, what about the documentation then. In such a situation, the trader, the employer, should transfer this documentation to the authorized keeper for payment, the present law regulates it, and then, of course, the trader no longer has to keep this documentation, of this obligation, in quotes, is released by transferring the documentation to the certified keeper and the responsibility for keeping that period to such a certified company. ParlaMint-PL_2018-01-09-sejm-55-1.u225	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! (PL) Mr President, I would like to ask you a few questions. I am pleased to present a report by the Legislative Commission on the draft law amending the Law on the special rules for the removal of legal effects of reprivatization decisions concerning Warsaw real estate, issued in violation of the law and certain other laws. The report is contained in print No 2132. The Law on the Specific Rules for the Removal of Legal Effects of Reprivatization Decisions provides the legal basis for the functioning of the Commission for the Removal of Legal Effects of Reprivatization Decisions on Warsaw Real Estate, issued in breach of the law. The first reading of the draft law on the amendment of this law took place at the 53rd meeting of the Sejm, where there was a request to reject this project at first reading. On 8 December 2017, the Sejm rejected this request and referred the above draft law to the Legislative Commission. The Legislative Commission considered this proposal at its meeting on 13 December 2017, namely last year. The meeting of the committee lasted almost 5 hours and, in addition to the members of the Legislative Commission, representatives of the government party and legislators took part in the meeting. Many amendments were tabled during the work on the project. The Commission accepted a number of drafting amendments and a number of substantive amendments to the draft submitted. The solutions adopted will enable the Commission to decide on the removal of the legal effects of reprivatisation decisions on the further effects of the reprivatization decision, which may form the basis for both the decision to establish the perpetual use of Warsaw real estate and the decision to transform the perpetual use of that property into property right. Those decisions may then, and should, unless they have irreparable legal effects, be repealed in the same procedure. This is due to the purpose of the committee, which is to reverse the effects of decisions taken in breach of the law. This amendment allows the verification committee to verify the subsequent decisions itself in the context of the case under consideration. The next amendment adopted by the Legislative Commission gives the possibility for the verification committee to impose an obligation on the person who, in bad faith, acquired ownership or right of perpetual use from the person for whom the reprivatisation decision was issued. In practice, this will allow for settlement with entities that purchased Warsaw real estate in bad faith from beneficiaries of reprivatization decisions issued in breach of law. Further amendments adopted give the basis for the reversal by decision of the committee, even if inextricable and unlawful, of the negative effects of the reprivatization decision consisting in the expulsion or eviction of Warsaw real estate tenants after the decision was taken in breach of the law for unauthorised persons. First of all, it imposes on the decision of the owner, m.st. Warsaw or the State Treasury, the obligation to restore to those affected by the actions of beneficiaries faulty decisions to have a dependent property or a relevant part of it, if these persons occupied the property, primarily rented premises, before issuing a reprivatization decision in breach of the law. The following article is inserted: 3a makes clear the application of the provisions on the protection of tenants of Warsaw property in respect of tenants and other persons dealing with real estate, also in cases of final reprivatization decisions before the date of entry into force of the Act. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The Commission also rejected a number of amendments which it considered unreasonable. There was also a broad discussion on the status of the members of the verification commission, and many proposals were made to resolve the existing problem. This is mainly the issue of insurance problems for those on this verification committee, both for Members and non-members. The subject was opened, but not tabled, and no specific amendment was tabled in writing. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The Legislative Commission asks the High Sejm to adopt the draft law annexed to the report and in accordance with the article. Article 43 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 3 The Rules of Procedure of the Sejm shall submit two minority applications at the request of the applicants. Thank you very much. I'll remind you here, Art. 64 Constitution, according to which the Republic protects property and this protection is equal for all, as well as Article. 21 of the Constitution, which states that the Republic protects property and expropriation is admissible only if it is carried out for public purposes and for fair compensation. Therefore, once again, contrary to the provisions of the Basic Law and contrary to common sense, you are pushing for solutions which have only political justification. Another of the proposed absurdities concerns the new rules of summoning the parties and witnesses to the hearing. Anyone who has ever been in contact with an office or court knows that if he is to appear, he receives by registered letter an appropriate call from which the content and details of this appointment are derived. The so-called verse, i.e. the receipt by the addressee, is proof that the consignment was actually delivered, and this is in the case file as evidence. And it's obvious, it's pretty obvious. This procedure takes a while, but at least it is certain that the witness has been informed of the case effectively. This obviousness is not so obvious to the Ministry of Justice. The summoning of witnesses to the hearing under the new rules will be carried out through the so-called. notices in the Public Information Bulletin on the website of the Ministry of Justice. In general, then, anyone who knows the person who bought the property in Warsaw in the last dozen years should check the website of the Ministry of Justice regularly. And the fork will be a witness because a friend bought an apartment on the ground that the developer purchased from someone who in the 1990s got this land as a result of a return decision. Moreover, after 7 days from the date of such announcement, there will be a presumption that such a witness has been effectively requested for this hearing. I understand that such methods can be applied to those who are already taking part in the proceedings and are aware that they can be called to this hearing, while the use of such a method actually in relation to a dozen million of our citizens creates an absolute legal absurdity and, as far as the future is concerned, also actual. ParlaMint-PL_2018-01-09-sejm-55-1.u247	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! The Committee on Justice and Human Rights considered the draft at its meetings on 12 and 13 December 2017 and the work in the committee was preceded by work in the subcommittee dealing with this particular project. I will briefly point out that the project crystallises the powers of the Central Authority, which is the Minister of Justice, from the point of view of the execution of several international acts, in particular the Hague Convention, which indicates the way in which the so-called. matters relating to parental authority, in international matters in this respect. The Act sets out solutions that accurately indicate the procedure that will apply in this respect. A number of changes aim to ensure that the welfare of children is taken into account in the proceedings and that it is guaranteed at every stage of the procedure. That's the way it is with this project. It indicates, first, the specific district courts which will consider these proceedings, very complex, requiring detailed knowledge and experience in this area. Secondly, it points to one appeal court which will deal with appeals, appeals against those judgments, orders at first instance. Importantly, the execution will only be subject to a final decision and, moreover, after consideration of the cassation action against the final decision. Moreover, the powers of the central authority are clearly defined from the initial point of view of the procedure, i.e. the receipt of the request, its formal verification, the notification of the parties and the activation of the entire mechanisms and tools to be used in order to enable proceedings to be carried out. On behalf of the Committee on Justice and Human Rights, of course, I strongly recommend this proposal. The Commission, of course, proposes that the draft be adopted in accordance with the report.
