ParlaMint-PL_2018-01-09-sejm-55-1.u248	Thank you, sir. Please take the floor of Mr. Mark Michalak's Child Rights Officer.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-01-09-sejm-55-1.u249	Dear Marshal, Minister! High Izbo! In view of the upcoming work of the Sejm on the Government's draft law on the performance of certain activities of the central authority in the field of family legal trade under European Union law and international agreements, I would like to share with the High Chamber the comments of the Ombudsman on the proposed regulations. I welcome the fact that this proposal is in line with many requests made by the Ombudsman for the rights of the child for 9 years. One of them was to restore the possibility to challenge the Supreme Court's final judgments based on the provisions of the Hague Convention on the Civil Aspects of the Abduction of a Child Abroad. I am very pleased about this because for many years I have been persistently seeking to restore the legal status before 2000 and to extend the catalogue of cases for which a cassation complaint in civil matters is entitled to be dealt with on the basis of the regulations contained in this international agreement. Starting in 2011, I directed six times the Advocate General's speeches first to the Minister of Justice, then to the President of the Republic of Poland, and then again to the Minister of Justice asking for legislative work to be taken on the amendment of the article. 519 § 2 Code of Civil Procedure. At the time, I pointed out that my observations showed that the courts, following the principle of speed of proceedings, did not sufficiently examine whether there were grounds for refusing a child to reside in the country of permanent residence in question - art. 13 lit. b of the Convention. I also argued that in the course of proceedings under the Hague Convention, in many cases there was a breach of substantive law by its misinterpretation or misuse. In the event of such shortcomings being detected at the stage of the proceedings, I reported the Ombudsman's involvement in the case in order to eliminate them. Unfortunately, such infringements were also disclosed after the judgment of the Court of First Instance, which could no longer be effectively challenged, since, under the applicable rules, there was no appeal against it. In my view, the introduction of the Judit Review of the Supreme Court in relation to the judgments given under the Hague Convention is a desirable and fully legitimate solution which will help to eliminate from the legal market faulty judicial decisions concerning such important issues as the country in which the child will reside. On the other hand, my concerns are raised by the proposed deadline for filing a cassation complaint in such cases. In my view, it is too short and inadequate for reality. According to the bill submitted, the Advocate General, the Ombudsman or the Ombudsman, will be able to file a cassation complaint within 2 months of the date of the order's validity. During the work of this draft law in the Committee on Justice and Human Rights, the Ombudsman pointed out the need to extend this period to at least 3 months from the date of the decision. As the Ombudsman of the rights of the child, I take the view that acceptance of the current proposal may make it much more difficult and even prevent the lodging of a cassation complaint because of, at least, the late notification of the persons entitled to file a valid case by the person concerned. In my experience, I also know that the mere transfer of the file by the court and their in-depth analysis require a time which, in the event of maintaining a two-month period for filing a cassation complaint, could simply be insufficient. Therefore, I ask you, ladies and gentlemen, to consider my proposal and to make an amendment which takes into account the extension of the deadline for lodging a cassation complaint to at least 3 months from the date on which the order was valid. Alternatively, I would like to consider the introduction into the draft law of the principle that a two-month period for lodging a cassation complaint will begin its course from the date of service to the party of the judgment with justification. Regardless of the above, I strongly hope that thanks to regulations such as those provided for in the draft law, the Polish courts, based on the judicial authority of the Supreme Court, will work out a uniform interpretation of the provisions of the Convention, in accordance with the best interests of the child. The lack of this instrument may in previous years have resulted in the legal circulation of defective decisions causing serious physical and mental damage to the minor's further psychophysical development as a result of the birth of a child from Poland. In my view, the single judicial line of the Supreme Court in matters of the Hague Convention will become a guarantee of legal certainty, protecting individuals from arbitrary decisions and contributing to the protection of the child's welfare. Another request of the Ombudsman of the rights of the child, which was reflected in the Government's draft law, is to waive the immediate enforceability of the provisions of the first instance in matters pending under the Hague Convention. The amendment is a response to the need to amend the Article, which I have repeatedly notified. 579 of the Civil Procedure Code, which governs the suspension of the enforceability of the provisions issued by the court of custody, by extending the catalogue of those provisions to a decision on the return of illegally kidnapped children to their habitual residence. I addressed my first speech to the Minister of Justice in 2009. The proposed amendment will protect children from the dramatic consequences of their parents' decisions, who were forced to hide with their children in anticipation of the final settlement of the case by the court of appeal without being able to count on the full duality of judicial proceedings. In such situations, children were often deprived of medical care, did not fulfil their schooling obligations, nor did they have any contact with their peers. In my view, the right to two-instant legal proceedings guaranteed in the Constitution of the Republic of Poland cannot be limited to its formal dimension - the lodging of an appeal. This law materializes in the possibility of a parent's real participation, but above all the child's participation in the ongoing proceedings in which their rights are decided. In the event of an unlawful decision being enforced, which could subsequently be repealed or amended as a result of a review by the Court of First Instance, the child is exposed to unnecessary travel to another country, often very distant geographically and culturally, to a change in the current environment, and sometimes to a loss of contact with the parent with whom he has a strong emotional relationship. Significantly, the enforceability of an unlawful judgment of the Court of First Instance which results in the return of the child to the country of permanent residence deprives the court of appeal of the possibility of supplementing the evidence with evidence from the hearing of the child, which has its source in Article. 72. 3 the Constitution of the Republic of Poland, or evidence from the opinions of the judicial experts. An example of the intervention of the Ombudsman on a case decided on the basis of the Hague Convention, in which the child was issued to the country of permanent residence on the basis of an unlawful decision of the Court of First Instance, is the case of the son of a Dutch citizen and a Polish citizen. By ordering the child's return to the Netherlands, the court required the mother to deliver the boy within 7 days of the decision. Although the minor's mother brought an appeal, it did not halt the enforceability of the order and did not prevent the forced removal of the child's mother and the transfer of it to the father with whom the minor returned to the Netherlands. A detailed analysis of the case file indicated that the evidence was incomplete, in particular because no evidence was obtained in the course of the proceedings at first instance to establish the current mental state of the boy. As a result, there was a situation in which the court of appeal was not able to personally carry out evidence of hearing the boy and knowing his position regarding his return to Poland or further residence in the Netherlands. In view of the above, I have notified the Ombudsman's participation in the case in question, requesting evidence from an expert psychologist's opinion. According to the above, the immediate feasibility of unlawful judgments in matters of the Hague Convention raises many problems of evidence and, most importantly, exposes the child to unwanted trauma. For these reasons, I was very pleased and satisfied that the projectors, taking into account the Ombudsman's repeated appeals on this subject for years, accepted the proposed solutions. I would like to express my gratitude to the Minister of Justice and to the members of the subcommittee and the Committee on Justice and Human Rights who are involved in the work on this draft law for agreeing to the proposed amendment of the article. 598 § 1 of the Code of Civil Procedure and the introduction of a rule limiting the application of compulsory suspension of proceedings only to situations where proceedings conducted under the Hague Convention are pending before designated courts in Poland. It is apparent from the observations made by the Ombudsman that the Polish courts, after having been informed of the opening of proceedings under the Hague Convention abroad, had doubts as to whether, in such a situation, they should suspend the care proceedings taking place in our country, or should proceed further with a view to a substantive examination of the case. Due to numerous complaints from parents questioning the practice of Polish courts, the issues raised have become of interest to the Ombudsman of the rights of the child and have been carefully analysed. For this purpose, I asked selected appeals courts in Poland to examine the above mentioned. issues and an indication of how in such cases the courts in the area of jurisdiction of individual appeals have ruled. The analysis of the information obtained from the different appeals courts showed that the rules contained in the Code of Civil Procedure were not uniform in conjunction with Article. 16 of the Hague Convention. The amendment also responds to the expectations of judges ruling in the family departments and minors who share the view that Article. 598 § 1 of the Civil Procedure Code is imprecise and does not clearly indicate whether it is a procedure under the Hague Convention in Poland or abroad. The clarification of this provision will resolve all doubts in this regard. To conclude my speech, I would like to stress that the proposed bill has the opportunity to contribute to the fuller protection of the rights of the child. As a spokesperson for the rights of the child, I believe that the new provisions that take into account the proposed proposals will not only be transparent and understandable, but will above all serve to protect the highest value - the good of the child. I ask you, ladies and gentlemen, to take over and report the amendments I have tabled that will improve the protection of the child's well-being, as reflected in the proposed draft law in print 1872. I ask the High House to pass it. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-01-09-sejm-55-1.u263	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the club of the Polish People's Party, I would like to present our position on the report of the committee on the government draft law on the performance of certain activities of the central authority in the field of legal trade in family matters under European Union law and international agreements, print Nos 1827 and 1927. The aim of this project is to improve the protection of the child's well-being and to improve the effectiveness of the enforcement of judicial decisions concerning his or her return abroad or of decisions refusing such return. The government project provides that in the case of a case for the issue of a child abroad it will remain in Poland at least until the appeal hearing. Judgments of the court will be executed as soon as they are enforced. At least because the proposal also introduces a cassation complaint against decisions to take the child away. It can be brought to the Supreme Court by the Attorney General, the Ombudsman and the Ombudsman of the Rights of the Child. Today, the provisions of the Children's Courts are enforced when they are announced, after the first instance, although the 1980 Hague Convention does not require this. Applications for the issue of children from Poland will be dealt with in the first instance by 11 specialised district courts having 6 weeks to issue a ruling. Also 6 weeks will have the Court of Appeal in Warsaw ruling in the second instance. Today, when regional courts are ruling in the first instance throughout the country, it takes more than a year - to the detriment of the stability of the child's life. Importantly, in regional courts, judges often meet with this category of cases for the first time. In all such cases, the parties will have to be represented by representatives, and when Polish parents cannot afford it, they will get it from the office, which will improve the procedures. It will also be possible to organise cross-border mediation between the parties. The Minister of Justice will be able to ask the courts to provide all information on the state of the proceedings and the courts will have to respond to him immediately. If an environmental interview is required, the courts will have 4 weeks to respond. The Polish Police will gain a new right - they will be able to collect and process, i.e. to use personal data of the persons concerned by the request to take the child away, and those who have kept them or hid them. The Treasury will cover the costs of translating applications submitted to foreign courts and institutions by Polish parents fighting for children when they prove that they cannot afford them. Unfortunately, this does not apply to Polish parents who are abroad and are taking legal action there. The powers of the Minister of Justice to take children away from Polish parents living in other countries by the authorities there will be much less. If a foreign court agrees that children taken from Poles will be transferred to a foster family in Poland, the minister will be able to direct applications to regional courts for the establishment of foster care in our country. The courts must respond within 21 days. After a positive response, the costs of bringing the child to our country will be able to cover the State Treasury. High Izbo! The changes envisaged in the project go in a positive direction for Polish children and their parents. The free movement of persons within the European Union is and will result in an increasing number of situations where the decision of the Community country where the child is to be raised falls into court. In this situation, the state's existing legal status and instruments in this area have not been sufficient. The transfer of this category of cases to 11 specialised district courts, together with the establishment of an appeal court, will help to harmonise the case-law line and improve proceedings. This should speed up and increase the professionalism of ruling in this area. It is also useful to assess the introduction of solicitor-radical coercion. It is good that in such sensitive matters the parties will be entitled to a representative appointed by the office. We stand in the position that wide and, if necessary, free access to justice and legal assistance should always guide the authors of changes in the Polish legal system. The enforcement of decisions only after the use of the appeal will avoid the creation of facts made in the form of sending a child abroad before the final outcome of the case. These changes are complemented by an extension of the rights to the acquisition and processing of personal data in the course of proceedings. This will allow for better discernment and better judgment on the part of the district courts. The Parliamentary Club of PSL declares its support for the contested bill.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-01-09-sejm-55-1.u264	In view of the fact that, at the time of the second reading, the draft amendment was tabled, I propose that the Sejm re-direct this proposal to the Committee on Justice and Human Rights in order to present a report. [[Adjournment from 10 a.m. 21 mins 10 h 21 min 20]] I'm reopening the session. We proceed with the examination of item 6 of the agenda: First reading of the government draft law amending the Act on the provision of environmental information and its protection, public participation in environmental protection and on environmental impact assessments and some other laws (Print No 2129). I ask the Undersecretary of State in the Ministry of the Environment, the chief conservation officer of nature of Mr Andrzej Szweda-Lewandowski, to present the justification for the bill.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-01-09-sejm-55-1.u265	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! In order to amend the Act on the provision of environmental information and its protection, public participation in environmental protection and environmental impact assessments, hereinafter referred to as u. o.o., it is necessary to introduce organisational changes in the structure of organisational units providing services to the Director General of Environmental Protection and Regional Environmental Directors in order to increase the efficiency of the activities of these bodies. Currently, the Director-General, GDOŚ, provides the Directorate-General for Environment, and the 16 regional environmental directors provide 16 regional environmental departments. Each of these units is a budgetary unit, which means that there are currently 17 separate state budget units, i.e. the general management and 16 regional directorates. Such a solution results in duplication of supporting processes in the Directorate-General and in the Regional Directorates, in particular in the field of financial management and human resources matters, and requires employment in each of these budgetary units of persons dealing with financial, human resources and administrative matters. As a result, service posts in the Directorate-General and Regional Directorates account for 18% all posts and up to 24% jobs in substance. At the same time, it should be stressed that, in a situation where new obligations are being imposed on regional directorates and general directorates, and that at present there are very many investments requiring environmental impact assessment procedures, these institutions are becoming less and less efficient. The continuity of proceedings in these institutions is not unique. Here it should be recalled that e.g. Change in U.O.C. the 2015 transfer to the Regional Environment Directorates of Administrative Procedure related to the issue of environmental decisions for municipalities and old age, which resulted in an increase in the number of generally conducted cases in the Regional Directorates of Procedure by more than 3000 in 2017 without any staff reinforcement. The flexible structure proposed in this project allows for a reduction or increase in the number of regional directorates without the need to liquidate or create new state budget units, which are currently regional directorates. In addition, it will allow the allocation of human resources and material resources according to needs, which will contribute to effective environmental and nature management. It will also ensure that the number of local units of each regional directorate can be adjusted to suit the needs. According to the Ministry, in the current situation, the structure of the local units of each regional directorate is not appropriate to the needs. In some directorates, she's overdeveloped. For example, the regional directorate in Poznań, which is territorially one of the largest, does not have any local unit, whereas the regional directorate in Lublin, which is one of the smallest territorially DOŚ, has as many as five local units. The proposed law gives the possibility to regulate such situations and to reduce, or possibly, in certain directorates, increase, depending on the needs, the number of local units. Designed changes in the creation of one specialized organizational unit will reduce the overdeveloped administrative apparatus. Consolidation of ancillary processes will allow the relocation of part of the posts that are intended to support these processes to carry out the substantive tasks. More substantive staff will allow faster handling of cases, in particular making decisions on environmental conditions, which will improve the implementation of investments in Poland. The proposed law does not introduce solutions unknown to the Polish legal system, because similar solutions apply in the national tax administration. The proposed law does not change the status of GDOŚ and WDOŚ. GDOŚ will remain a central government body and retain the existing competences. Similarly, the WDOŚ will continue to remain an area-based body of non-integrated administration and will retain the existing competences. Nor will institutional jurisdiction change - GDOŚ will remain an appeal body against decisions issued by WDOŚ at first instance. There will also be no change in the procedural rules, in particular those governing environmental impact assessments. Thank you.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-01-09-sejm-55-1.u266	Thank you, Minister. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. Mr Jerzy Gosievski, Law and Justice have the floor.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-01-09-sejm-55-1.u267	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! Dear Minister, On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I have the honour to present a position on the Government draft law amending the Act on the provision of environmental information and its protection, public participation in environmental protection and environmental impact assessments and certain other laws, together with the draft implementing act, print No 2129. The proposed law provides for the simplification of the organisational structure of offices serving GDOŚ and WDOŚ by setting up a state organizational unit specialising in environmental protection and nature protection, so-called. Directorate for Environmental Protection - DOS, which is a national budgetary unit, whose manager will be the General Director for Environmental Protection. He will perform his tasks with his deputies. As proposed, the Environment Directorate will include the headquarters of the Environment Directorate and regional branches. The head office of the Environment Directorate and the regional branches of the Environment Directorate will be in the structure of one budgetary unit, i.e. the Environment Directorate. A statute setting out the organisation of the Environment Directorate will be established by the Minister responsible for Environment. The Minister responsible for Environmental Affairs, when issuing the statute of the Directorate for Environmental Protection, will be able to identify organisational cells, departments and cells equivalent to the headquarters of the Directorate for Environmental Protection, which will be headed by senior officials in the civil service, as well as local departments in the regional departments of the DOS. The proposed solution will lead to the consolidation at the level of the Environment DG of all the ancillary processes to be implemented at the headquarters of the Environment Directorate and regional departments of the Environment Directorate. This applies primarily to financial and human resources management, asset management, repair and investment, public procurement, IT, audit, control, quality management, communication, legally protected information and health and safety. As a result, and entrusting the management of funds to the Director-General for Environmental Protection, this will lead to a real increase in the economic efficiency of environmental and nature authorities. In addition, consolidation of supporting processes will benefit the efficiency and speed of work in the Environment Directorate, as the General Environment Director will be able, depending on legitimate needs, to carry out more flexible management of human resources and financial and tangible assets, as well as to focus activities on priority tasks to protect the environment and nature. The transfer of issues in the field of local economy and staff matters to the DOJ will therefore relieve the WDOŚ and allow them to focus even more on carrying out statutory environmental and nature protection tasks. The proposed solution will not reduce the capacity of the public administration to apply European Union rules effectively, in particular in the field of environmental impact assessments, but will contribute to the even more efficient performance of its tasks, and in particular by shifting jobs to substantive tasks described above. The work of the Committee on the Environment, Natural Resources and Forestry will clarify the provisions of the proposed amendments and consideration will also be given to the possibility of amendments attached to the draft regulation, which will result in e.g. Considered opportunity to maintain regional environmental management in Warmia and Mazury, which have huge natural potential. The proposed changes aim to improve the functioning of the system. The proposals for institutional changes will strengthen the effectiveness and effectiveness of the regional environmental DGs and the DG Environment. Parliamentary Club Law and Justice supports the draft law on amending the Act on the provision of environmental information and its protection, public participation in environmental protection and on environmental impact assessments and certain other laws contained in Seym Paper No 2129. Thank you very much. [[♪ The bell ♪]]

One of the most important elements that this law contains is the possibility of submitting applications for direct and area payments under the RDP in an electronic version. The proposal in an electronic version has been changing for several years as a call for agricultural environments, but the efficiency of the IT system of the restructuring and modernisation agencies has not so far given the possibility of introducing this method of applying directly to farmers. Both representatives of the restructuring and modernisation agencies and representatives of the Ministry have ensured that there is no threat to the construction, extension and modernisation of the IT system, which will enable farmers who submit applications online. The system that has been operated so far - the employees of the restructuring and modernisation agencies had access to it - allowed only about 600 users to access it at the same time. On the other hand, the new system, which is currently being prepared by the Agency, will at the same time allow access to applications to tens of thousands of beneficiaries, which would definitely relieve the agencies' county offices and ensure quick and efficient submission of applications by farmers. However, given that not all farmers - these questions were also raised in the committee - have access to computers, not everyone has the possibility or ability to use them efficiently, for 3 years more farmers will receive all those annexes that are necessary to complete the application, and their electronic introduction will help both the employees of the county offices as well as the employees of agricultural advisory centres, agricultural chambers and private advisory services, which are also developing in Poland. Moreover, the Ministry of Agriculture ensures that a large information campaign will be carried out, explaining how these applications are to be submitted. This is actually a historic event. We're going through, you could say, like Kazimierz the Great from wooden construction to brick construction. This year, which is also enshrined in the Act, farmers who do not have one year to change direct payments will not have to submit an electronic application. They will be able to make a statement on a special form made available by the restructuring and modernisation agency that there is no change in the amount of the farm's payments. And such a statement is enough to trigger payments for farmers. These statements will be made one month before the statutory deadline, which starts on 15 March, from 15 February to 14 March. This will greatly facilitate and speed up the work of the restructuring and modernisation agencies, as these statements can already be analysed and examined by the agency a month earlier, which is extremely important. Of course, if someone wants to make any changes, they will also be able to make such changes in this statement by May. It is also important that we change the threshold for investments carried out under the RDP, which so far required that procedures be used to select competitive contractors from the amount of 20,000. zł, which is, you have to say, it was a low threshold. The Act amends this threshold similar to that in the Public Procurement Act, in the Public Procurement Law, up to 30 000. Euros. So this is a leaping and very important increase in the threshold, as farmers also expected. I hope that few farmers will benefit from this, but in a situation where the farmer, for reasons independent of himself or herself, related to death or illness or to other unforeseen accidents in the family and on the farm, will not be able to submit an electronic application, in justified cases under this law, the head of the county office of the restructuring and modernisation agencies will be able to agree to submit an application in paper form. This law is indeed a very important step in digitising Polish agriculture, in communicating farmers with the paying agency through the use of IT opportunities. The discussions I had with farmers show that the reasons for this should be seen primarily in the lack of clear information and technical assistance from the institution and in the failure of the application. Today, at first reading, we heard that work is still underway on the preparation of the application. There can be a great deal of problems with the operation of an IT system serving a million-dollar number of beneficiaries. For example, the efficiency of the application: the highest number of payment applications per year is submitted in the last days of the deadline, which could lead to system failure and even block the possibility of an effective application. I would like to stress that the introduction of an obligation to make electronic applications for direct payments is a late step, but undoubtedly very good, not only because of the considerable facilitation and saving of time for farmers, but also because of the positive impact on digitization of the Polish village. However, implementation is more important than a good direction. I therefore call on the Ministry of Agriculture, the Agency for Restructuring and Modernisation of Agriculture and other institutions under the Ministry, which will be responsible for implementing the application, to provide farmers with the support and assistance necessary to ensure that this task is carried out with the utmost care due to the short time limit. ParlaMint-PL_2018-01-09-sejm-55-1.u319	Mr. Marshal! High Seym! I would like to present the position of the Freedom and Solidarnia Circle on the Government bill amending the Payment Act under direct support schemes and certain other laws. The proposed law introduces the principle of submitting applications for direct payments and area payments from the Rural Development Programme, the RDPs, in electronic form. The aim is to make all applications for these payments electronically over time. In addition, the proposed amendment will make it possible to make a statement in which the farmer will confirm that there are no changes compared to the 2017 proposal. To date, a new application for direct payments had to be submitted. This means simplifying the procedure for applying for direct payments. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: Point 3 (5) of the draft law proposes amendments which will lead to an increase in the threshold above which applicants for aid or technical assistance and beneficiaries of the scheme will be required to apply the principles of competitive selection of contractors of tasks included in the in-kind and financial statement of operations from 20 000. PLN up to 30,000. the euro, i.e. up to the same threshold as for public procurement. It was also possible for designated entities and beneficiaries to determine only one criterion for assessing tenders - price or cost. A further amendment to be made pursuant to Article. Point 5a of the proposed law is to add to the legal order of the RDPs 2014,2020 provisions enabling applicants for or technical assistance and beneficiaries of the scheme: 1. the conclusion of a contract for the performance of a given task included in the in-kind-financial statement of the operation with the contractor selected under certain conditions, where the best offer cannot be chosen as a result of the selection procedure carried out for the contractor of the task included in that combination; 2. the conclusion of a contract with the contractor whose tender was the next most advantageous of the other tenders, where, following the selection procedure carried out, the contractor of a given task included in the performance statement of the operation in kind and in kind, the selected contractor is refused the conclusion of the contract. The Free and Solidarni Circle supports the proposed changes that will simplify the procedures for granting direct payments and area payments from the Rural Development Programme.
